    50	// Any git subcommand that could mutate refs/worktrees/working-tree. On EVERY
    51	// refusal path the full recorded call list must contain none of these.
    52	const DESTRUCTIVE = /\b(branch\s+-[dD]\b|reset\b|clean\b|checkout\b|restore\b|rebase\b|merge\b|worktree\s+remove\b|push\b|commit\b)/;
    53	function assertNoDestructiveGit(git, label) {
    54	  const bad = git.git().filter((c) => DESTRUCTIVE.test(c.argstr));
    55	  assert.deepEqual(bad.map((c) => c.argstr), [], `${label}: no destructive git may be issued on a refusal path`);
    56	}
    57	
    58	/**
    59	 * A recording fake git. `responses` maps an args-substring (longest match wins)
    60	 * to a canned result; { reject:true } models a nonzero git exit. An UNSTUBBED
    61	 * git invocation REJECTS (ENOSTUB) — a test must declare its command surface.
    62	 */
    63	function fakeGit(responses = {}, lockProbe = null) {
    64	  const keys = Object.keys(responses).sort((a, b) => b.length - a.length);
    65	  const calls = /** @type {any[]} */ ([]);
    66	  const fn = (/** @type {string} */ cmd, /** @type {string[]} */ args = [], /** @type {any} */ opts = {}) => {
    67	    const argstr = args.join(' ');
    68	    // Record whether the merge lock is present AT CALL TIME (finding 2): proves
    69	    // mutating git ran under the acquired lock.
    70	    calls.push({ cmd, args, argstr, cwd: opts?.cwd, lockPresent: typeof lockProbe === 'function' ? lockProbe() : undefined });
    71	    if (cmd !== 'git') return Promise.reject(Object.assign(new Error('spawn ENOENT'), { code: 'ENOENT' }));
    72	    const k = keys.find((key) => argstr.includes(key));
    73	    if (k === undefined) return Promise.reject(Object.assign(new Error(`unstubbed git: ${argstr}`), { code: 'ENOSTUB' }));
    74	    const r = responses[k];
    75	    if (r?.reject) return Promise.reject(Object.assign(new Error(r.stderr || 'git failed'), { code: r.code ?? 1, stdout: r.stdout ?? '', stderr: r.stderr ?? '' }));
    76	    return Promise.resolve({ stdout: r?.stdout ?? '', stderr: r?.stderr ?? '' });
    77	  };
    78	  fn.calls = calls;
    79	  fn.git = () => calls.filter((c) => c.cmd === 'git');
    80	  fn.issued = (/** @type {RegExp} */ re) => calls.some((c) => c.cmd === 'git' && re.test(c.argstr));
    81	  fn.withCwd = (/** @type {RegExp} */ re, /** @type {string} */ cwd) => calls.filter((c) => c.cmd === 'git' && re.test(c.argstr) && c.cwd === cwd);
    82	  fn.matching = (/** @type {RegExp} */ re) => calls.filter((c) => c.cmd === 'git' && re.test(c.argstr));
    83	  return fn;
    84	}

    87	// file at each call time.
    88	const MERGE_LOCK = '/repo/.handoff/loop/merge.lock';
    89	function mergeRepo(responses = {}, files = {}) {
    90	  const io = makeIo({
    91	    now: '2026-07-19T00:00:00.000Z',
    92	    host: 'wt-host',
    93	    pid: 4242,
    94	    startTime: 111000,
    95	    files: { '/repo/.git/HEAD': 'ref: refs/heads/main\n', ...files },
    96	  });
    97	  const git = fakeGit(responses, () => io.fs.existsSync(MERGE_LOCK));
    98	  io.execFile = git;
    99	  io.__git = git;
   100	  return { io, git };
   101	}

   103	// git worktree list --porcelain output for the given entries.
   104	function porcelain(entries) {
   105	  return entries.map((e) => `worktree ${e.path}\nHEAD ${e.sha ?? MAIN_SHA}\nbranch refs/heads/${e.branch}\n`).join('\n');
   106	}
   107	
   108	function makeRepo({ git, files = {} } = {}) {
   109	  const io = makeIo({
   110	    now: '2026-07-19T00:00:00.000Z',
   111	    host: 'wt-host',
   112	    pid: 4242,
   113	    startTime: 111000,
   114	    files: { '/repo/.git/HEAD': 'ref: refs/heads/main\n', ...files },
   115	  });
   116	  if (git) io.execFile = git;
   117	  io.__git = git;
   118	  return io;
   119	}

   245	    assertNoDestructiveGit(git, 'preflight wrong-branch');
   246	  });

   303	describe('worktrees — attribution range scan (the merge gate)', () => {
   304	  const BRANCH = 'baton/wt-a/subtask-1';
   305	  const RANGE = `main..${BRANCH}`;
   306	  const cleanLog = logStdout(REC('c1', NAME, EMAIL, NAME, EMAIL, 'subtask: add tests'), REC('c2', NAME, EMAIL, NAME, EMAIL, 'subtask: implement'));
   307	
   308	  it('a clean range (sole author+committer, no forbidden trailers) passes', async () => {
   309	    const { attributionScan } = M();

   311	    const io = makeRepo({ git });

   313	    assert.equal(r.ok, true);
   314	  });
   315	
   316	  it('requests a NUL-field + RS-record log format carrying sha/author(name,email)/committer(name,email)/body over the FULL range (no last-N)', async () => {
   317	    const { attributionScan } = M();
   318	    const git = fakeGit({ [`log ${RANGE}`]: { stdout: cleanLog } });
   319	    const io = makeRepo({ git });
   320	    await attributionScan('/repo', BRANCH, io);
   321	    const logCall = git.git().find((c) => c.argstr.startsWith('log '));
   322	    assert.ok(logCall, 'a git log was issued');
   323	    assert.match(logCall.argstr, new RegExp('main\\.\\.baton/wt-a/subtask-1'), 'the scan uses the exact range');
   324	    // The EXACT order-preserving format the fixtures are built from — parser and
   325	    // fixture derive from the one LOG_FORMAT constant, so they cannot diverge.
   326	    assert.ok(logCall.argstr.includes(LOG_FORMAT), `the scan requests the exact format ${LOG_FORMAT}; got: ${logCall.argstr}`);
   327	    assert.ok(!/\s-n\s|--max-count/.test(logCall.argstr), 'the scan is NOT bounded to a last-N window');
   328	  });
   329	
   330	  // Mismatch-only negatives — exactly one identity field wrong per case (finding 3).
   331	  const IDENTITY_CASES = [
   332	    ['author NAME', REC('id-an', 'Wrong Name', EMAIL, NAME, EMAIL, 'x'), /author|name|identity/i],
   333	    ['author EMAIL', REC('id-ae', NAME, 'wrong@x.z', NAME, EMAIL, 'x'), /author|email|identity/i],
   334	    ['committer NAME', REC('id-cn', NAME, EMAIL, 'Wrong CN', EMAIL, 'x'), /committer|name|identity/i],
   335	    ['committer EMAIL', REC('id-ce', NAME, EMAIL, NAME, 'wrong@x.z', 'x'), /committer|email|identity/i],
   336	  ];
   337	  for (const [label, rec, reasonRe] of IDENTITY_CASES) {
   338	    it(`a mismatched ${label} (all other identity fields valid) blocks, naming the offending commit`, async () => {
   339	      const { attributionScan } = M();
   340	      const git = fakeGit({ [`log ${RANGE}`]: { stdout: logStdout(rec) } });
   341	      const io = makeRepo({ git });
   342	      const r = await attributionScan('/repo', BRANCH, io);
   343	      assert.equal(r.ok, false, `a mismatched ${label} must block`);
   344	      assert.match(String(r.offending), new RegExp(String(rec).split('\x00')[0]), 'names the offending commit');
   345	      assert.match(String(r.reason), reasonRe, `the reason names the ${label} violation`);
   346	      assertNoDestructiveGit(git, `attribution ${label}`);
   347	    });
   348	  }

   350	  it('a foreign AUTHOR blocks, naming the offending commit', async () => {

   393	// ===========================================================================
   394	describe('worktrees — merge transaction', () => {
   395	  const BRANCH = 'baton/wt-a/subtask-1';
   396	  const RANGE = `main..${BRANCH}`;
   397	  const cleanLog = logStdout(REC('c1', NAME, EMAIL, NAME, EMAIL, 'clean'));
   398	
   399	  it('refuses to merge while a merge-lock is held (no destructive git at all), leaving the owner lock intact', async () => {
   400	    const { mergeSubtask } = M();
   401	    const held = JSON.stringify({ host: 'wt-host', pid: 55555 });
   402	    const { io, git } = mergeRepo({}, { [MERGE_LOCK]: held }); // any stubbed git call would be a violation
   403	    const r = await mergeSubtask('/repo', { branch: BRANCH }, io);
   404	    assert.equal(r.ok, false);
   405	    assert.match(String(r.reason), /lock|held|merge/i, 'the refusal names the held merge lock');
   406	    assertNoDestructiveGit(git, 'held-lock refusal');
   407	    assert.equal(io.files()[MERGE_LOCK], held, "another owner's lock is left intact");
   408	  });
   409	
   410	  it('a merge CONFLICT aborts (git merge --abort), parks, and RELEASES the lock', async () => {
   411	    const { mergeSubtask } = M();
   412	    const { io, git } = mergeRepo({
   413	      [`log ${RANGE}`]: { stdout: cleanLog },
   414	      [`merge ${BRANCH}`]: { reject: true, code: 1, stdout: 'CONFLICT (content): Merge conflict in src/x.js\nAutomatic merge failed' },
   415	      'merge --abort': { stdout: '' },
   416	    });
   417	    const r = await mergeSubtask('/repo', { branch: BRANCH }, io);
   418	    assert.equal(r.ok, false);
   419	    // The conflicting merge ran at the repo root, under the acquired lock.
   420	    const merges = git.matching(new RegExp(`^merge ${BRANCH.replace(/\//g, '\\/')}$`));
   421	    assert.equal(merges.length, 1, 'exactly one merge attempt');
   422	    assert.equal(merges[0].cwd, '/repo', 'the merge ran at the repo root');
   423	    assert.equal(merges[0].lockPresent, true, 'the merge ran under the acquired lock');
   424	    // The abort also ran at the repo root, still under the lock.
   425	    const aborts = git.matching(/^merge --abort$/);
   426	    assert.equal(aborts.length, 1, 'the conflicted merge is aborted exactly once');
   427	    assert.equal(aborts[0].cwd, '/repo', 'the abort runs at the repo root (where the merge happened)');
   428	    assert.equal(aborts[0].lockPresent, true, 'the abort runs under the acquired lock');
   429	    assert.match(String(r.reason), /conflict|park/i, 'the result is a park-shaped refusal');
   430	    assert.ok(!git.issued(/commit|-X (ours|theirs)|checkout --theirs/), 'no auto-resolution was attempted');
   431	    assert.ok(!io.fs.existsSync(MERGE_LOCK), 'the merge lock is released after the conflict abort');
   432	  });
   433	
   434	  it('a clean merge runs `merge <branch>` in /repo UNDER the lock, ff-syncs `merge --ff-only main` once per seat cwd (also under the lock), releases the lock', async () => {
   435	    const { mergeSubtask } = M();
   436	    const { io, git } = mergeRepo({
   437	      [`log ${RANGE}`]: { stdout: cleanLog },
   438	      [`merge ${BRANCH}`]: { stdout: 'Updating; Fast-forward' },
   439	      'merge --ff-only main': { stdout: 'Already up to date.' },
   440	    });
   441	    const r = await mergeSubtask('/repo', { branch: BRANCH }, io);
   442	    assert.equal(r.ok, true);
   443	
   444	    // The merge itself: exactly one, in /repo, while the lock is held.
   445	    const merges = git.matching(new RegExp(`^merge ${BRANCH.replace(/\//g, '\\/')}$`));
   446	    assert.equal(merges.length, 1, 'exactly one merge of the subtask branch');
   447	    assert.equal(merges[0].cwd, '/repo', 'the merge into main runs at the repo root');
   448	    assert.equal(merges[0].lockPresent, true, 'the merge ran under the acquired lock');
   449	
   450	    // ff-only sync: EXACTLY 'merge --ff-only main' (anchored, no trailing tokens),
   451	    // once per seat cwd, under the lock.
   452	    const ffA = git.withCwd(/^merge --ff-only main$/, WT_A);
   453	    const ffB = git.withCwd(/^merge --ff-only main$/, WT_B);
   454	    assert.equal(ffA.length, 1, 'wt-a fast-forwards to main in its own cwd');
   455	    assert.equal(ffB.length, 1, 'wt-b fast-forwards to main in its own cwd');
   456	    assert.equal(git.matching(/^merge --ff-only/).length, 2, 'exactly two ff-only syncs, one per seat');
   457	    assert.ok(ffA[0].lockPresent && ffB[0].lockPresent, 'the ff-only syncs ran under the acquired lock');
   458	
   459	    assert.ok(!io.fs.existsSync(MERGE_LOCK), 'the merge lock is released after a successful merge');
   460	  });
   461	
   462	  it('a NON-fast-forward worktree sync is a corruption signal → refusal, and RELEASES the lock', async () => {
   463	    const { mergeSubtask } = M();
   464	    const { io } = mergeRepo({
   465	      [`log ${RANGE}`]: { stdout: cleanLog },
   466	      [`merge ${BRANCH}`]: { stdout: 'Fast-forward' },
   467	      'merge --ff-only main': { reject: true, code: 1, stderr: 'fatal: Not possible to fast-forward, aborting.' },
   468	    });
   469	    const r = await mergeSubtask('/repo', { branch: BRANCH }, io);
   470	    assert.equal(r.ok, false);
   471	    assert.match(String(r.reason), /fast-forward|ff|corrupt|sync/i, 'a non-ff sync refuses as corruption');
   472	    assert.ok(!io.fs.existsSync(MERGE_LOCK), 'the merge lock is released on a non-ff refusal');
   473	  });
   474	
   475	  it('an attribution violation BLOCKS the merge (no destructive git) and RELEASES the lock', async () => {
   476	    const { mergeSubtask } = M();
   477	    const bad = logStdout(REC('badc0de', 'Someone Else', 'x@y.z', NAME, EMAIL, 'sneaky'));
   478	    const { io, git } = mergeRepo({ [`log ${RANGE}`]: { stdout: bad } });
   479	    const r = await mergeSubtask('/repo', { branch: BRANCH }, io);
   480	    assert.equal(r.ok, false);
   481	    assert.match(String(r.reason), /author|attribution|identity/i, 'the merge is gated on the attribution scan');
   482	    assertNoDestructiveGit(git, 'attribution-block refusal');
   483	    assert.ok(!io.fs.existsSync(MERGE_LOCK), 'the merge lock is released even when attribution blocks after acquisition');
   484	  });
   485	});

   488	describe('worktrees — self-heal and teardown', () => {
   489	  it('a raw-deleted worktree dir (listed by git, missing on fs) is pruned then re-added', async () => {
   490	    const { selfHealWorktree } = M();
   491	    const git = fakeGit({
   492	      'worktree list': { stdout: porcelain([{ path: '/repo', branch: 'main' }, { path: WT_A, branch: 'baton/wt-a/base' }]) },
   493	      'worktree prune': { stdout: '' },
   494	      'worktree add': { stdout: '' },
   495	    });
   496	    const io = makeRepo({ git }); // no files under WT_A -> fs says it is gone
   497	    const r = await selfHealWorktree('/repo', { seat: 'a', branch: 'baton/wt-a/base' }, io);
   498	    assert.equal(r.healed, true);
   499	    assert.ok(git.issued(/worktree prune/), 'the stale metadata is pruned');
   500	    assert.ok(git.issued(/worktree add/), 'the worktree is re-added');
   501	  });
   502	
   503	  it('teardown removes worktrees + deletes ONLY baton/wt- branches, never a non-baton branch', async () => {
   504	    const { teardownWorktrees } = M();
   505	    const git = fakeGit({
   506	      'worktree list': {
   507	        stdout: porcelain([
   508	          { path: '/repo', branch: 'main' },
   509	          { path: WT_A, branch: 'baton/wt-a/base' },
   510	          { path: WT_B, branch: 'baton/wt-b/base' },
   511	          { path: '/repo/.worktrees/other', branch: 'feature/keep-me' },
   512	        ]),
   513	      },
   514	      'worktree remove': { stdout: '' },
   515	      'branch -D': { stdout: '' },
   516	    });
   517	    const io = makeRepo({ git });
   518	    const r = await teardownWorktrees('/repo', io);
   519	    assert.equal(r.ok, true);
   520	
   521	    // Exactly the two seat worktrees are removed — never the non-baton one.
   522	    const removes = git.matching(/worktree remove/).map((c) => c.argstr);
   523	    assert.ok(removes.some((s) => s.includes(WT_A)), 'wt-a worktree removed');
   524	    assert.ok(removes.some((s) => s.includes(WT_B)), 'wt-b worktree removed');
   525	    assert.equal(removes.length, 2, 'exactly two worktree removes — the non-baton worktree is untouched');
   526	    assert.ok(!removes.some((s) => /other/.test(s)), 'a non-baton worktree is never removed');
   527	
   528	    // Exactly the two listed baton branches are deleted — no extras.
   529	    const deleted = git.matching(/branch -D/).map((c) => c.argstr);
   530	    assert.ok(deleted.some((s) => /branch -D baton\/wt-a\/base\b/.test(s)), 'baton/wt-a/base deleted');
   531	    assert.ok(deleted.some((s) => /branch -D baton\/wt-b\/base\b/.test(s)), 'baton/wt-b/base deleted');
   532	    assert.equal(deleted.length, 2, 'exactly two branch deletions — no extra deletions');
   533	    assert.ok(!deleted.some((s) => /feature\/keep-me/.test(s)), 'a non-baton branch is never deleted');
   534	
   535	    // The result records both seats.
   536	    assert.deepEqual([...(r.removed ?? [])].sort(), ['a', 'b'], 'res.removed contains both seats');
   537	  });
   538	});

   283	describe('pipeline — seat alternation (writer)', () => {
   284	  it('subtask 1 writes in wt-a on baton/wt-a/subtask-t1 (worker-a model); subtask 2 writes in wt-b on baton/wt-b/subtask-t2 (worker-b model)', async () => {
   285	    const io = makePipeRepo({ runner: undefined });
   286	    io.superviseChild = fakeRunner(io, [...cleanSubtask(), ...cleanSubtask()]);
   287	    io.__runner = io.superviseChild;
   288	    const code = await run(['pipeline', 'run'], io);
   289	    assert.equal(code, 0);
   290	
   291	    // Branch creation recorded in each seat's own cwd.
   292	    assert.equal(io.__git.withCwd(/^checkout -b baton\/wt-a\/subtask-t1$/, WT_A).length, 1, 'subtask 1 branch created in wt-a');
   293	    assert.equal(io.__git.withCwd(/^checkout -b baton\/wt-b\/subtask-t2$/, WT_B).length, 1, 'subtask 2 branch created in wt-b');
   294	
   295	    // The WRITERS are exactly the two write-capable children, in subtask order —
   296	    // isolating by -s workspace-write means a read-only reviewer can never
   297	    // satisfy this (finding 1).
   298	    const writers = writersOf(io.__runner);

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	/**
     2	 * `baton pipeline run` — the Layer-3 dual-worktree preset over the loop
     3	 * engine. Subtasks alternate writer seats (index 0 → wt-a/worker-a, 1 →
     4	 * wt-b/worker-b, …); the reviewer runs READ-ONLY from the OTHER seat with
     5	 * that seat's worker model under the subtask-reviewer role; the merger child
     6	 * (read-only) adversarially re-checks, and on its APPROVED the SUPERVISOR
     7	 * merges through worktrees.mergeSubtask (attribution-gated, locked, ff-only
     8	 * seat syncs). Plan: docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md
     9	 * §"baton pipeline (Layer 3 — a loop preset)".
    10	 */
    11	import {
    12	  loopPaths,
    13	  initLoopState,
    14	  applyLoopEvent,
    15	  writeLoopState,
    16	  appendLoopEvent,
    17	  LOOP_EVENT,
    18	  LOOP_STATUS,
    19	} from '../loop/state.mjs';
    20	import { buildChildArgv, superviseChild } from '../loop/children.mjs';
    21	import { setupWorktrees, preflightWorktree, postflightWorktree, mergeSubtask, worktreePaths } from '../loop/worktrees.mjs';
    22	import { resolveRoles } from '../roles/resolve.mjs';
    23	import { loadConfig } from '../roles/matrix.mjs';
    24	import { atomicWriteText, ensureDir, safeReadJson } from '../util/fsx.mjs';
    25	import { emitEnvelope, usageError, parseFlagsStrict, resolveRoot } from './shared.mjs';
    26	
    27	const STRING_FLAGS = new Set(['root']);
    28	const EXIT_ESCALATED = 3;
    29	const EXIT_PARKED = 4;
    30	
    31	/**
    32	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
    33	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
    34	 */
    35	export async function cmdPipeline(args, io) {
    36	  /** @type {string[]} */
    37	  const positionals = [];
    38	  /** @type {string[]} */
    39	  const flagTokens = [];
    40	  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i += 1) {
    41	    const a = args[i];
    42	    if (typeof a === 'string' && a.startsWith('--')) {
    43	      flagTokens.push(a);
    44	      if (STRING_FLAGS.has(a.slice(2)) && i + 1 < args.length) {
    45	        i += 1;
    46	        flagTokens.push(args[i]);
    47	      }
    48	    } else {
    49	      positionals.push(a);
    50	    }
    51	  }
    52	  const parsed = parseFlagsStrict(flagTokens, {});
    53	  if (parsed.error !== undefined) return usageError(io, parsed.flags, 'pipeline', parsed.error);
    54	  const flags = parsed.flags;
    55	
    56	  const sub = positionals[0];
    57	  if (sub === undefined) return usageError(io, flags, 'pipeline', 'a subcommand is required — try: baton pipeline run');
    58	  if (sub !== 'run') return usageError(io, flags, 'pipeline', `unknown subcommand '${sub}' (supported: run)`);
    59	  if (positionals.length > 1) return usageError(io, flags, 'pipeline', `unexpected argument '${positionals[1]}'`);
    60	
    61	  return runPipeline(flags, io);
    62	}
    63	
    64	/** Validate the spec's subtasks — every rejection names its offender.
    65	 * @param {any} subtasks @returns {string | null} */
    66	function validateSubtasks(subtasks) {
    67	  if (!Array.isArray(subtasks) || subtasks.length === 0) {
    68	    return 'loop.json needs a non-empty `subtasks` array ([{id, title}]) for a pipeline run';
    69	  }
    70	  const seen = new Set();
    71	  for (let i = 0; i < subtasks.length; i += 1) {
    72	    const st = subtasks[i];
    73	    if (typeof st?.id !== 'string' || st.id.length === 0) return `subtasks[${i}] is missing a string id`;
    74	    if (typeof st?.title !== 'string' || st.title.length === 0) return `subtasks[${i}] ('${st.id}') is missing a string title`;
    75	    if (seen.has(st.id)) return `subtasks carry a duplicate id '${st.id}' — ids name branches and must be unique`;
    76	    seen.add(st.id);
    77	  }
    78	  return null;
    79	}
    80	
    81	/**
    82	 * @param {Record<string, string | boolean>} flags @param {any} io
    83	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
    84	 */
    85	async function runPipeline(flags, io) {
    86	  const root = resolveRoot(io, flags);
    87	  const p = loopPaths(root);
    88	
    89	  const rawSpec = safeReadJson(io.fs, `${root}/loop.json`);
    90	  if (!rawSpec.ok) return usageError(io, flags, 'pipeline', `no loop.json at ${root} — scaffold one with: baton loop init "<goal>"`);
    91	  const spec = rawSpec.value;
    92	  const subtaskError = validateSubtasks(spec?.subtasks);
    93	  if (subtaskError !== null) return usageError(io, flags, 'pipeline', subtaskError);
    94	  const { config } = loadConfig(root, io);
    95	  if (!config) return usageError(io, flags, 'pipeline', `no readable baton.config.json at ${root} — the role matrix is required`);
    96	  const cap = typeof spec?.budgets?.iterationCap === 'number' ? spec.budgets.iterationCap : 5;
    97	  const timeoutMs = (typeof spec?.budgets?.perRoleTimeoutMin === 'number' ? spec.budgets.perRoleTimeoutMin : 30) * 60_000;
    98	
    99	  /** @param {string} role @returns {any | null} */
   100	  const resolveOne = (role) => {
   101	    const a = resolveRoles({ config, to: 'claude-code', avoid: [], avoidEntries: [], probes: null }).assignments[role];
   102	    return a && a.mode !== 'unavailable' ? a : null;
   103	  };
   104	
   105	  // The synthesized phase list makes the loop state machine (phase count,
   106	  // done detection, journal replay) work unchanged for the subtask cycle.
   107	  const subtasks = spec.subtasks;
   108	  const stateSpec = {
   109	    goal: spec.goal,
   110	    phases: subtasks.map((/** @type {any} */ st, /** @type {number} */ i) => ({ id: `subtask-${st.id}`, role: `worker-${i % 2 === 0 ? 'a' : 'b'}` })),
   111	  };
   112	  /** @type {any} */
   113	  let state = initLoopState(stateSpec, io);
   114	  await writeLoopState(root, state, io);
   115	  const runner = io.superviseChild ?? superviseChild;
   116	  const seats = worktreePaths(root).seats;
   117	  let childSeq = 0;
   118	
   119	  const transition = async (/** @type {any} */ ev) => {
   120	    const next = applyLoopEvent(state, ev);
   121	    const seq = await appendLoopEvent(root, ev, io);
   122	    state = { ...next, journalSeq: seq };
   123	    await writeLoopState(root, state, io);
   124	    return state;
   125	  };
   126	  const park = async (/** @type {string} */ reason) => {
   127	    await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.PARK, reason });
   128	    io.stderr.write(`baton pipeline run: parked — ${reason}\n`);
   129	    return EXIT_PARKED;
   130	  };
   131	
   132	  await setupWorktrees(root, io);
   133	
   134	  const spawn = async (/** @type {any} */ assignment, /** @type {string} */ prompt, /** @type {string} */ seatPath, /** @type {string} */ label) => {
   135	    childSeq += 1;
   136	    const logPath = `${p.dir}/children/${String(childSeq).padStart(3, '0')}-${label}.log`;
   137	    ensureDir(io.fs, `${p.dir}/children`);
   138	    const childSpec = buildChildArgv(assignment, prompt, { root: seatPath });
   139	    return runner(childSpec, { timeoutMs, graceMs: 10_000, logPath, maxLogBytes: 1_000_000, platform: assignment.platform });
   140	  };
   141	
   142	  for (let i = 0; i < subtasks.length; i += 1) {
   143	    const st = subtasks[i];
   144	    const seat = i % 2 === 0 ? 'a' : 'b';
   145	    const other = seat === 'a' ? 'b' : 'a';
   146	    const branch = `baton/wt-${seat}/subtask-${st.id}`;
   147	    const gate = `subtask-${st.id}-review`;
   148	
   149	    const writerAssignment = resolveOne(`worker-${seat}`);
   150	    const reviewerModel = resolveOne(`worker-${other}`);
   151	    const mergerModel = resolveOne('merger');
   152	    if (!writerAssignment || !reviewerModel || !mergerModel) {
   153	      return park(`no eligible assignment for subtask '${st.id}' (worker-${seat}/worker-${other}/merger must all resolve)`);
   154	    }
   155	
   156	    // Start the subtask branch in the writer's seat, then verify the seat.
   157	    await io.execFile('git', ['checkout', '-b', branch], { cwd: seats[seat] });
   158	    const pre = await preflightWorktree(root, { seat, branch }, io);
   159	    if (pre.ok !== true) return park(`preflight refused subtask '${st.id}': ${pre.refusal}`);
   160	    const mainSha = (await io.execFile('git', ['rev-parse', 'main'], { cwd: root })).stdout.trim();
   161	
   162	    let findings = '';
   163	    let merged = false;
   164	    while (!merged) {
   165	      if ((state.iterations?.[gate] ?? 0) >= cap) {
   166	        await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.GATE_ITERATION, gate, verdict: 'BLOCKED' });
   167	        atomicWriteText(
   168	          io.fs,
   169	          `${p.dir}/ESCALATION.md`,
   170	          `# Pipeline escalation\n\nGate '${gate}' exhausted its ${cap}-iteration cap.\nLast findings:\n\n${findings}\n`,
   171	        );
   172	        io.stderr.write(`baton pipeline run: gate '${gate}' hit the ${cap}-iteration cap — escalated (see ${p.dir}/ESCALATION.md)\n`);
   173	        return EXIT_ESCALATED;
   174	      }
   175	
   176	      // Writer (write-capable, its own seat).
   177	      const writerPrompt = [
   178	        `You are the writer (worker-${seat}) for subtask '${st.id}': ${st.title}.`,
   179	        `Goal: ${spec.goal}. Work ONLY on branch ${branch} in your worktree; commit your work there.`,
   180	        findings ? `The previous attempt was BLOCKED — address every finding:\n${findings}` : '',
   181	      ]
   182	        .filter(Boolean)
   183	        .join('\n\n');
   184	      await spawn({ ...writerAssignment, role: `worker-${seat}` }, writerPrompt, seats[seat], `${st.id}-writer`);
   185	
   186	      const post = await postflightWorktree(root, { seat, branch, mainSha }, io);
   187	      if (post.ok !== true) return park(`postflight refused subtask '${st.id}': ${post.refusal}`);
   188	
   189	      // Reviewer (read-only, the OTHER seat's model and cwd, subtask-reviewer role).
   190	      const reviewPrompt = [
   191	        `You are the subtask-reviewer for subtask '${st.id}': ${st.title}.`,
   192	        `Review the diff of ${branch} against main from the ${other} seat (fresh context).`,
   193	        "End with exactly:\nVERDICT: APPROVED | APPROVED_WITH_NOTES | BLOCKED\nFINDINGS: numbered findings, or 'none'",
   194	      ].join('\n\n');
   195	      const review = await spawn({ ...reviewerModel, role: 'subtask-reviewer' }, reviewPrompt, seats[other], `${st.id}-review`);
   196	      if (review.verdict !== 'APPROVED' && review.verdict !== 'APPROVED_WITH_NOTES') {
   197	        findings = String(review.findings ?? '');
   198	        const after = await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.GATE_ITERATION, gate, verdict: 'BLOCKED' });
   199	        if (after.status === LOOP_STATUS.ESCALATED) {
   200	          atomicWriteText(io.fs, `${p.dir}/ESCALATION.md`, `# Pipeline escalation\n\nGate '${gate}' exhausted its ${cap}-iteration cap.\nLast findings:\n\n${findings}\n`);
   201	          return EXIT_ESCALATED;
   202	        }
   203	        continue;
   204	      }
   205	
   206	      // Merger (read-only child — it re-checks; the SUPERVISOR merges).
   207	      const mergerPrompt = [
   208	        `You are the merger for subtask '${st.id}'. Adversarially re-check ${branch} against main before it merges.`,
   209	        "End with exactly:\nVERDICT: APPROVED | APPROVED_WITH_NOTES | BLOCKED\nFINDINGS: numbered findings, or 'none'",
   210	      ].join('\n\n');
   211	      const mergerVerdict = await spawn({ ...mergerModel, role: 'merger' }, mergerPrompt, root, `${st.id}-merge-check`);
   212	      if (mergerVerdict.verdict !== 'APPROVED' && mergerVerdict.verdict !== 'APPROVED_WITH_NOTES') {
   213	        findings = String(mergerVerdict.findings ?? '');
   214	        const after = await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.GATE_ITERATION, gate, verdict: 'BLOCKED' });
   215	        if (after.status === LOOP_STATUS.ESCALATED) {
   216	          atomicWriteText(io.fs, `${p.dir}/ESCALATION.md`, `# Pipeline escalation\n\nGate '${gate}' exhausted its ${cap}-iteration cap.\nLast findings:\n\n${findings}\n`);
   217	          return EXIT_ESCALATED;
   218	        }
   219	        continue;
   220	      }
   221	
   222	      const merge = await mergeSubtask(root, { branch }, io);
   223	      if (merge.ok !== true) return park(`merge of '${branch}' refused: ${merge.reason}`);
   224	      merged = true;
   225	    }
   226	
   227	    await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.PHASE_ADVANCE });
   228	  }
   229	
   230	  if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { status: state.status, subtasks: subtasks.length } });
   231	  else io.stdout.write(`baton pipeline run: done — ${subtasks.length} subtask(s) merged\n`);
   232	  return state.status === LOOP_STATUS.DONE ? 0 : state.status === LOOP_STATUS.PARKED ? EXIT_PARKED : 0;
   233	}

    80	/**
    81	 * A STRICT, stateful fake git: unstubbed commands reject; worktree add/list and
    82	 * checkout -b are modeled so setup + preflight/postflight + merge cohere.
    83	 * `over` overrides specific responses (e.g. a merge conflict).
    84	 */
    85	function pipelineGit({ over = {}, log = CLEAN_LOG } = {}) {
    86	  const added = /** @type {Map<string,string>} */ (new Map()); // path -> branch
    87	  const branchAt = /** @type {Map<string,string>} */ (new Map()); // cwd -> current branch
    88	  const calls = /** @type {any[]} */ ([]);
    89	  const lockProbeRef = { fn: /** @type {null | (() => boolean)} */ (null) };
    90	  let mainSha = MAIN_SHA; // mutable so a child can "move main" mid-subtask
    91	
    92	  const list = () => {
    93	    const entries = [{ path: '/repo', branch: 'main' }];
    94	    for (const [path, branch] of added) entries.push({ path, branch: branchAt.get(path) ?? branch });
    95	    return entries.map((e) => `worktree ${e.path}\nHEAD ${MAIN_SHA}\nbranch refs/heads/${e.branch}\n`).join('\n');
    96	  };
    97	
    98	  const fn = (/** @type {string} */ cmd, /** @type {string[]} */ args = [], /** @type {any} */ opts = {}) => {
    99	    const argstr = args.join(' ');
   100	    calls.push({ cmd, args, argstr, cwd: opts?.cwd, lockPresent: lockProbeRef.fn ? lockProbeRef.fn() : undefined });
   101	    if (cmd !== 'git') return Promise.reject(Object.assign(new Error('spawn ENOENT'), { code: 'ENOENT' }));
   102	
   103	    // Explicit overrides win.
   104	    const okey = Object.keys(over).sort((a, b) => b.length - a.length).find((k) => argstr.includes(k));
   105	    if (okey) {
   106	      const r = over[okey];
   107	      if (r?.reject) return Promise.reject(Object.assign(new Error(r.stderr || 'git failed'), { code: r.code ?? 1, stdout: r.stdout ?? '', stderr: r.stderr ?? '' }));
   108	      return Promise.resolve({ stdout: r?.stdout ?? '', stderr: r?.stderr ?? '' });
   109	    }
   110	
   111	    if (argstr.startsWith('worktree list')) return Promise.resolve({ stdout: list(), stderr: '' });
   112	    if (argstr.startsWith('worktree add')) {
   113	      const path = args.find((a) => a.startsWith('/repo/.worktrees/'));
   114	      const bi = args.indexOf('-b');
   115	      const branch = bi >= 0 ? args[bi + 1] : 'baton/wt-x/base';
   116	      if (path) { added.set(path, branch); branchAt.set(path, branch); }
   117	      return Promise.resolve({ stdout: '', stderr: '' });
   118	    }
   119	    if (argstr.startsWith('worktree prune') || argstr.startsWith('worktree remove')) return Promise.resolve({ stdout: '', stderr: '' });
   120	    if (argstr.startsWith('checkout -b')) { if (opts?.cwd) branchAt.set(opts.cwd, args[args.indexOf('-b') + 1]); return Promise.resolve({ stdout: '', stderr: '' }); }
   121	    if (argstr.startsWith('status --porcelain')) return Promise.resolve({ stdout: '', stderr: '' });
   122	    if (argstr === 'rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD') return Promise.resolve({ stdout: `${branchAt.get(opts?.cwd) ?? 'main'}\n`, stderr: '' });
   123	    if (argstr.startsWith('rev-parse')) return Promise.resolve({ stdout: `${mainSha}\n`, stderr: '' });
   124	    if (argstr.startsWith('log ')) return Promise.resolve({ stdout: log, stderr: '' });
   125	    if (argstr.startsWith('merge')) return Promise.resolve({ stdout: 'Fast-forward', stderr: '' });
   126	    if (argstr.startsWith('branch -D')) return Promise.resolve({ stdout: '', stderr: '' });
   127	    return Promise.reject(Object.assign(new Error(`unstubbed git: ${argstr}`), { code: 'ENOSTUB' }));
   128	  };
   129	  fn.calls = calls;
   130	  fn.git = () => calls.filter((c) => c.cmd === 'git');
   131	  fn.issued = (/** @type {RegExp} */ re) => calls.some((c) => c.cmd === 'git' && re.test(c.argstr));
   132	  fn.matching = (/** @type {RegExp} */ re) => calls.filter((c) => c.cmd === 'git' && re.test(c.argstr));
   133	  fn.withCwd = (/** @type {RegExp} */ re, /** @type {string} */ cwd) => calls.filter((c) => c.cmd === 'git' && re.test(c.argstr) && c.cwd === cwd);
   134	  fn.setLockProbe = (/** @type {() => boolean} */ f) => { lockProbeRef.fn = f; };
   135	  fn.moveMain = (/** @type {string} */ sha) => { mainSha = sha; }; // a child "moves main" mid-subtask
   136	  fn.setBranch = (/** @type {string} */ cwd, /** @type {string} */ branch) => { branchAt.set(cwd, branch); };
   137	  return fn;
   138	}

   140	function fakeRunner(io, script) {
   141	  const queue = [...script];
   142	  const calls = /** @type {any[]} */ ([]);
   143	  const fn = (/** @type {any} */ spec, /** @type {any} */ opts = {}) => {
   144	    calls.push({ spec, opts, command: spec?.command, args: spec?.args ?? [], env: spec?.env });
   145	    const r = queue.shift() ?? { verdict: 'APPROVED' };
   146	    if (typeof r.logContent === 'string' && opts.logPath) {
   147	      const dir = opts.logPath.slice(0, opts.logPath.lastIndexOf('/'));
   148	      io.fs.mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
   149	      io.fs.writeFileSync(opts.logPath, r.logContent);
   150	    }
   151	    // A child may perturb the world (e.g. move main) between spawn and postflight.
   152	    if (typeof r.effect === 'function') r.effect();
   153	    return Promise.resolve({ timedOut: false, exitCode: r.exitCode ?? 0, verdict: r.verdict ?? 'APPROVED', findings: r.findings ?? '', logPath: opts.logPath });
   154	  };
   155	  fn.calls = calls;
   156	  return fn;
   157	}

   176	  io.execFile = g;
   177	  const fake = runner ?? fakeRunner(io, []);
   178	  io.superviseChild = fake;
   179	  io.__git = g;
   180	  io.__runner = fake;

     1	/**
     2	 * Pure, explainable role resolution over the committed matrix. Every decision
     3	 * is auditable: chosen entries carry their chainIndex; passed-over entries land
     4	 * in skipped[] with a why. avoid[] population is the caller's job.
     5	 * @param {{config: any, to: string, avoid?: string[], avoidEntries?: Array<{platform: string, model: string}>, nativeOnly?: boolean, probes?: Record<string, {capability: string, outcome: string}> | null}} input
     6	 * @returns {{assignments: Record<string, any>, notes: string[]}}
     7	 */
     8	export function resolveRoles({ config, to, avoid = [], avoidEntries = [], nativeOnly = false, probes = null }) {
     9	  /** @type {string[]} */
    10	  const notes = [];
    11	
    12	  /**
    13	   * The eligibility verdict for a platform, or null when eligible.
    14	   * @param {string} platform @returns {string | null}
    15	   */
    16	  function skipReason(platform) {
    17	    if (avoid.includes(platform)) return 'avoided';
    18	    if (!(platform in (config.platforms ?? {}))) return 'unknown-platform';
    19	    if (config.platforms[platform]?.enabled === false) return 'disabled';
    20	    if (probes && probes[platform]) {
    21	      const { capability, outcome } = probes[platform];
    22	      if (outcome === 'rate-limited') return 'rate-limited';
    23	      if (capability === 'not-installed') return 'not-installed';
    24	      // 'installed' means installation was PROVEN but the probe was capped there
    25	      // (capOnSuccess) — authentication is UNVERIFIED, not verified-failed. Only
    26	      // a probe that ran and did NOT succeed (outcome !== 'ok') is a verified
    27	      // auth/reachability failure worth skipping; a capped success stays
    28	      // selectable and is flagged degraded below (iter-3 F1, plan §Role matrix).
    29	      if (capability === 'installed' && outcome !== 'ok') return 'unauthenticated';
    30	    }
    31	    return null;
    32	  }
    33	
    34	  // A selection is degraded when its availability is UNVERIFIED (gate-2 iter-2
    35	  // M3, iter-3 F1): with no probe cache at all, resolution is offline and EVERY
    36	  // selection is degraded (plan: "Offline resolution always succeeds, flagged
    37	  // degraded"); with a partial cache, platforms the cache is missing are; and a
    38	  // platform probed only to 'installed' (auth unverified, e.g. a --version
    39	  // success) is degraded even though it stays selectable.
    40	  /** @param {string} platform */
    41	  const probeUnverified = (platform) => {
    42	    if (probes == null || !probes[platform]) return true;
    43	    const { capability, outcome } = probes[platform];
    44	    // Degraded when ANY required dimension is unverified: a capped 'installed'
    45	    // (auth never confirmed) OR a non-ok outcome (iter-4 I6) — a selectable
    46	    // platform whose last probe ERRORED or TIMED OUT is presented as
    47	    // reachability-verified otherwise, which is exactly the silent-degradation
    48	    // the plan's degraded flag exists to surface. (rate-limited is skipped, not
    49	    // selected, so it never reaches here.)
    50	    return capability === 'installed' || outcome !== 'ok';
    51	  };
    52	
    53	  // Entry-level avoidance (plan §Model-level failover): a specific
    54	  // {platform, model} tuple is dead (e.g. account-tier rejected) while its
    55	  // platform stays usable. Matching is on model IDENTITY — an @effort suffix
    56	  // on either side is ignored — and requires BOTH dimensions, so the same
    57	  // model name on another platform is never caught.
    58	  /** @param {any} m */
    59	  const baseModel = (m) => (typeof m === 'string' ? m.split('@')[0] : m);
    60	  /** @param {string} platform @param {string} model */
    61	  const entryAvoided = (platform, model) =>
    62	    avoidEntries.some((a) => a?.platform === platform && baseModel(a?.model) === baseModel(model));
    63	
    64	  /** @type {Record<string, any>} */
    65	  const assignments = {};
    66	  for (const [role, chain] of Object.entries(config.roles)) {
    67	    /** @type {any[]} */
    68	    const skipped = [];
    69	    /** @type {any} */
    70	    let assignment = null;
    71	
    72	    for (let i = 0; i < chain.length; i += 1) {
    73	      const entry = chain[i];
    74	      if (nativeOnly && entry.platform !== to) {
    75	        skipped.push({ chainIndex: i, platform: entry.platform, model: entry.model, why: 'non-native' });
    76	        continue;
    77	      }
    78	      const why = skipReason(entry.platform);
    79	      if (why) {
    80	        skipped.push({ chainIndex: i, platform: entry.platform, model: entry.model, why });
    81	        continue;
    82	      }
    83	      // After skipReason so a dual match keeps the platform-level label.
    84	      if (entryAvoided(entry.platform, entry.model)) {
    85	        skipped.push({ chainIndex: i, platform: entry.platform, model: entry.model, why: 'avoided-entry' });
    86	        continue;
    87	      }
    88	      assignment = {
    89	        platform: entry.platform,
    90	        model: entry.model,
    91	        effort: entry.effort ?? null,
    92	        mode: entry.platform === to ? 'native' : 'delegated',
    93	        chainIndex: i,
    94	        skipped,
    95	      };
    96	      if (probeUnverified(entry.platform)) assignment.degraded = true;
    97	      break;
    98	    }
    99	
   100	    if (!assignment && nativeOnly) {
   101	      // Forced-default is only valid when the destination itself is eligible.
   102	      const whyTo = skipReason(to);
   103	      const model = config.defaults?.[to];
   104	      if (!whyTo && typeof model === 'string') {
   105	        assignment = { platform: to, model, effort: null, mode: 'forced-default', chainIndex: null, skipped };
   106	        if (probeUnverified(to)) assignment.degraded = true;
   107	      }
   108	    }
   109	
   110	    if (!assignment) {
   111	      assignment = { platform: null, model: null, effort: null, mode: 'unavailable', chainIndex: null, skipped };
   112	    }
   113	    assignments[role] = assignment;
   114	  }
   115	
   116	  return { assignments, notes };
   117	}

    23	//             sessionHint, probes, avoid, avoidEntries, attempt, loopState,
    24	//             degradedOpen? }

    26	//     prior limit death left the bundle unsealed). runFailover then SKIPS
    27	//     finalize and takes receive's degraded-open path (no .finalize.json freeze;
    28	//     the receive generates a degraded seal). The default alive path checkpoints
    29	//     -> finalize (seals) -> sealed receive.

    31	//     retry     -> { action:'retry', class, attempt }

    33	//     relaunch  -> { action:'relaunch', class, assignment, avoidEntries?, prompt? }
    34	//
    35	// Window drift (finding 5) is expressed purely through io.execFile SEQUENCING
    36	// (git rev-parse HEAD returning different values on successive calls) — the
    37	// production receive path re-derives git for prepare and for commit, so a HEAD
    38	// change between them stales the token. There is NO test-only hook.

   138	const parsedConfig = (io) => loadConfig('/repo', io).config;
   139	const asg = (platform, role, model, effort = null) => ({ platform, role, model, effort, mode: platform === 'codex' ? 'native' : 'delegated' });
   140	
   141	// A git execFile whose HEAD is chosen per rev-parse-HEAD call (the drift seam),
   142	// and which counts those calls so a test can pin the exact number of
   143	// prepare->commit windows. gitSnapshot reads clean status/diffs.
   144	function gitExec(headForCall) {
   145	  let headCalls = 0;
   146	  const fn = (/** @type {string} */ cmd, /** @type {string[]} */ args = []) => {
   147	    if (cmd !== 'git') return Promise.reject(Object.assign(new Error('spawn ENOENT'), { code: 'ENOENT' }));
   148	    const key = args.join(' ');
   149	    if (key === 'rev-parse HEAD') {
   150	      headCalls += 1;
   151	      return Promise.resolve({ stdout: headForCall(headCalls) + '\n', stderr: '' });
   152	    }
   153	    const out = key === 'rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD' ? 'main' : '';
   154	    return Promise.resolve({ stdout: out + '\n', stderr: '' });
   155	  };
   156	  fn.headCalls = () => headCalls;
   157	  return fn;
   158	}

   160	// Spy every write touching .handoff/loop.
   161	function spyLoopWrites(io) {
   162	  const writes = /** @type {string[]} */ ([]);
   163	  const note = (/** @type {any} */ p) => { if (typeof p === 'string' && p.startsWith('/repo/.handoff/loop')) writes.push(p); };
   164	  for (const m of ['writeFileSync', 'appendFileSync', 'mkdirSync', 'unlinkSync', 'rmSync']) {
   165	    const real = io.fs[m].bind(io.fs);
   166	    io.fs[m] = (/** @type {any} */ p, /** @type {any} */ a) => { note(p); return real(p, a); };
   167	  }
   168	  for (const m of ['renameSync', 'copyFileSync']) {
   169	    const real = io.fs[m].bind(io.fs);
   170	    io.fs[m] = (/** @type {any} */ from, /** @type {any} */ to) => { note(from); note(to); return real(from, to); };
   171	  }
   172	  return writes;
   173	}

   175	const historyFreezes = (io) => {
   176	  const dir = bundlePaths('/repo').historyDir;
   177	  if (!io.fs.existsSync(dir)) return [];
   178	  // Parse ONLY snapshot freezes (.json). rotateJournalIn also writes .ndjson
   179	  // journal freezes here — legitimately empty or multi-line — which are not JSON.
   180	  return io.fs
   181	    .readdirSync(dir)
   182	    .filter((/** @type {string} */ n) => n.endsWith('.json'))
   183	    .map((/** @type {string} */ n) => ({ name: n, data: JSON.parse(io.fs.readFileSync(`${dir}/${n}`, 'utf8')) }));
   184	};

   329	  });
   330	
   331	  it('(a) alive-supervisor open path: checkpoint -> finalize -> SEALED receive, NO degraded flag', async () => {
   332	    const { runFailover } = M();
   333	    const io = makeRepoIo({ status: 'open' });
   334	    await runFailover(usageLimitInput(io)); // default: the supervisor can seal
   335	    const freezes = historyFreezes(io);
   336	    const fin = freezes.find((f) => /\.finalize\.json$/.test(f.name));
   337	    assert.ok(fin && fin.data.handoff.status === 'sealed', 'the alive path seals via a .finalize.json freeze');
   338	    const received = freezes.find((f) => f.data.handoff && f.data.handoff.status === 'received');
   339	    assert.ok(received, 'commit reaches a received seal');
   340	    assert.doesNotMatch(JSON.stringify(received.data.handoff.receive_log ?? []), /degraded/i, 'a sealed->received commit is NOT flagged degraded');
   341	  });
   342	
   343	  it('(b) degraded-open path: finalize skipped (degradedOpen) -> NO .finalize.json, a DEGRADED receive seal', async () => {
   344	    const { runFailover } = M();
   345	    const io = makeRepoIo({ status: 'open' });
   346	    // The supervisor could not cleanly seal (prior limit death left the bundle
   347	    // unsealed); runFailover skips finalize and takes receive's degraded-open path.
   348	    const d = await runFailover(usageLimitInput(io, { degradedOpen: true }));
   349	    const freezes = historyFreezes(io);
   350	    assert.ok(!freezes.some((f) => /\.finalize\.json$/.test(f.name)), 'no finalize freeze — the seal was skipped');
   351	    const received = freezes.find((f) => f.data.handoff && f.data.handoff.status === 'received');
   352	    assert.ok(received, 'an unsealed limit-death still reaches a received seal (degraded-open path)');
   353	    assert.match(JSON.stringify(received.data.handoff.receive_log ?? []), /degraded[-_ ]?seal|degradedSeal/i, 'the open->received commit is flagged degraded');
   354	    assert.equal(loadBundle('/repo', io).bundle.generation, 2, 'the degraded receive still opened a fresh generation');
   355	    assert.equal(d.action, 'relaunch');
   356	  });

   389	describe('runFailover — usage-limit: git drift between prepare and commit (e), via io.execFile', () => {
   390	  // Drift tests seed an ALREADY-SEALED bundle and pass no new loopState, so the
   391	  // only git consumers are receive's prepare + commit — each HEAD read maps 1:1
   392	  // onto a prepare/commit call, with no leading finalize/checkpoint git.
   393	  const driftInput = (io) => usageLimitInput(io, { loopState: undefined });
   394	
   395	  it('git moves ONCE between prepare and its commit -> first commit stale -> ONE re-prepare+commit succeeds', async () => {
   396	    const { runFailover } = M();
   397	    // calls: 1=prepare(A) 2=commit(B, differs -> stale) 3=re-prepare(stable) 4=re-commit(stable, matches)
   398	    const git = gitExec((n) => (n === 1 ? 'HEAD-A' : n === 2 ? 'HEAD-B' : 'HEAD-STABLE'));
   399	    const io = makeRepoIo({ status: 'sealed', execFile: git });
   400	    const d = await runFailover(driftInput(io));
   401	    assert.equal(d.action, 'relaunch', 'the single automatic retry recovers from the one-time drift');
   402	    assert.equal(loadBundle('/repo', io).bundle.generation, 2, 'the retried commit landed');
   403	  });
   404	
   405	  it('git keeps moving every window -> SECOND consecutive stale -> {action:park}, EXACTLY two prepare/commit windows', async () => {
   406	    const { runFailover } = M();
   407	    // Every call a new HEAD: commit always differs from its own prepare -> stale.
   408	    const git = gitExec((n) => `HEAD-${n}`);
   409	    const io = makeRepoIo({ status: 'sealed', execFile: git });
   410	    const d = await runFailover(driftInput(io));
   411	    assert.equal(d.action, 'park', 'two consecutive stale commits park instead of looping');
   412	    assert.match(String(d.reason), /stale|drift|re-?prepare/i, 'the park reason names the drift');
   413	    assert.equal(loadBundle('/repo', io).bundle.generation, 1, 'no generation opened when the failover parked');
   414	    // Retry cap is EXACTLY one: two prepare+commit windows = 4 HEAD reads; a
   415	    // bounded-3+ loop would read 6+ (finding 3).
   416	    assert.equal(git.headCalls(), 4, 'exactly two prepare->commit windows were attempted (one retry, then park)');
   417	  });
   418	});
   419

   421	describe('runFailover — usage-limit: total exhaustion (g)', () => {

    19	// reason, and produces a correct remapped resume prompt — contract-tested in all
    20	// six directions"; §Concurrency "six-direction contract tests cover sealed +
    21	// unsealed variants"; constraint 6: dual writers, provably-dead takeover,
    22	// competing receivers, prepare-without-commit, chained failover; docs/design/
    23	// core.md §integration test list).
    24	//
    25	// TARGET MODULE: core/src/commands/init.mjs (sole unimplemented dependency and
    26	// this file's declared target — its absence is the only reason this file is
    27	// RED). init is the first step of the real failover flow; every other module the
    28	// e2e drives (checkpoint, finalize, receive, store, lock, txn, git/snapshot) is
    29	// already implemented, so once init lands the whole file runs green.
    30	//
    31	// This suite runs over the REAL fs (mkdtemp) with a REAL git repo (where the
    32	// flow needs one) and a purpose-built real-fs `io` (node:fs + a promisified
    33	// execFile), NOT the memfs fake — the point is to exercise atomic writes,
    34	// journal replay, locks, and git re-derivation against a real filesystem.
    35	// Unit-level equivalents of some concurrency cases exist in tests/unit/
    36	// lock.test.mjs and tests/unit/receive-txn.test.mjs over memfs; the versions
    37	// here re-exercise those CONTRACTS through the real fs and the real command
    38	// layer, asserting outcomes (exit codes, on-disk tree bytes), not memfs
    39	// internals.
    40	//
    41	// PINS (documented so the contract is explicit where the plan left room):
    42	//   E1. "Golden-pinned resume prompt" is realized as PINNED CONTENT (the goal,
    43	//       the claims-not-instructions posture line, the HANDOFF.md pointer, the
    44	//       origin/reason, and the REMAPPED role platforms), plus the <=2500 cap.
    45	//       The byte-exact prompt FORMAT is already golden-pinned by
    46	//       tests/unit/receive-prompt.test.mjs; pinning bytes again here is brittle
    47	//       against a real git sha / timestamps, so this file pins the failover
    48	//       FACTS instead (correct remap, dead origin avoided).
    49	//   E2. FAILOVER INVARIANT (universal, every direction): when the switch reason
    50	//       classifies as usage-limit for the origin, NO role remaps back onto the
    51	//       dead origin platform. "At least one role resolves to the target" is
    52	//       asserted only where the committed config guarantees it (the resolver
    53	//       uses nativeOnly:false at receive, so a target with no earlier-eligible
    54	//       chain entry can legitimately yield zero target rows).
    55	//   E3. The controlled baton.config.json (the methodology 7-role matrix) is
    56	//       written into the repo AFTER init so the remap is deterministic and
    57	//       decoupled from init's template content; init-scaffold correctness is
    58	//       asserted separately (a valid config + a covering .gitignore land).
    59	//   E4. Per-platform usage-limit reason strings come from core/data/
    60	//       signatures.v1.json so classifyReason yields usage-limit for the origin
    61	//       (claude-code "session limit", codex "usage limit", cursor regex).
    62	//   E5. SIX-DIRECTION MATRIX (verifier fold F1): each of the 6 directions runs
    63	//       over BOTH a sealed and an open (unsealed limit-death) bundle, through
    64	//       prepare AND commit, asserting: no dead-origin remap; the open variant
    65	//       warns "unsealed" at prepare and its archived freeze carries the

    67	//       seal recording intake origin/reason, flagged `degraded-seal`" — matched
    68	//       tolerantly as /degraded[-_ ]?seal|degradedSeal/); exactly one 'receive'
    69	//       freeze whose handoff.status is 'received'; the live bundle opens

   107	const pexec = promisify(realExecFile);
   108	const tmpDirs = [];
   109	
   110	/** A real-fs io honoring the injected-io contract (docs/design/core.md). */
   111	function makeRealIo(cwd, { stdin = '', now = NOW, processAlive } = {}) {
   112	  const out = [];
   113	  const err = [];
   114	  let tokenN = 0;
   115	  return {
   116	    cwd,
   117	    env: { ...process.env },
   118	    stdin,
   119	    stdout: { write: (s) => (out.push(String(s)), true) },
   120	    stderr: { write: (s) => (err.push(String(s)), true) },
   121	    fs: nodeFs,
   122	    execFile: (cmd, args = [], opts = {}) => pexec(cmd, args, { ...opts, encoding: 'utf8' }),
   123	    now: () => now,
   124	    host: 'e2e-host',
   125	    pid: 4242,
   126	    startTime: 111000,
   127	    processAlive: processAlive || ((pid) => pid === 4242),
   128	    // Small retry budget (gate-2 fix 12): the live-lock refusal fixture below
   129	    // pins the drop-after-bounded-wait behavior without the 3 s production wait.
   130	    lockRetry: { attempts: 2, delayMs: 5 },
   131	    newFencingToken: () => `e2e-tok-${(tokenN += 1)}`,
   132	    stdoutText: () => out.join(''),
   133	    stderrText: () => err.join(''),
   134	  };
   135	}
   136	
   137	function mkTmp(prefix) {
   138	  const dir = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), prefix));
   139	  tmpDirs.push(dir);
   140	  return dir;
   141	}
   142	
   143	function git(cwd, args) {
   144	  const r = spawnSync('git', args, { cwd, encoding: 'utf8' });
   145	  if (r.status !== 0) throw new Error(`git ${args.join(' ')} failed: ${r.stderr || r.stdout}`);
   146	  return r.stdout;
   147	}

   149	/** A fresh real git repo with one commit and no AI attribution anywhere. */
   150	function initRepo(cwd) {
   151	  git(cwd, ['init', '-q']);
   152	  git(cwd, ['config', 'user.email', 'e2e@example.test']);
   153	  git(cwd, ['config', 'user.name', 'baton e2e']);
   154	  git(cwd, ['config', 'commit.gpgsign', 'false']);
   155	  nodeFs.writeFileSync(join(cwd, 'README.md'), '# demo\n');
   156	  git(cwd, ['add', '.']);
   157	  git(cwd, ['commit', '-q', '-m', 'initial']);
   158	}

   161	  JSON.stringify({ schema: 'baton/event@1', dedupeKey, sessionHint, unstable, ...obj });

   163	/** 25 pre-normalized baton/event@1 checkpoints with unique dedupe keys. */
   164	function buildEvents() {
   165	  const e = [];
   166	  e.push(evt({ type: 'task.update', payload: { goal: GOAL, constraints: ['Deterministic output'], acceptance: ['survives kill-mid-write'] } }, 'e2e-goal'));
   167	  e.push(evt({ type: 'plan.set', payload: { steps: [
   168	    { id: 's1', title: 'Init scaffold', status: 'pending', note: null },
   169	    { id: 's2', title: 'Run the checkpoint loop', status: 'pending', note: null },
   170	    { id: 's3', title: 'Finalize and receive', status: 'pending', note: null },
   171	    { id: 's4', title: 'Prove chained failover', status: 'pending', note: null },
   172	  ] } }, 'e2e-plan'));
   173	  e.push(evt({ type: 'plan.step', payload: { id: 's1', status: 'done' } }, 'e2e-s1'));
   174	  for (let i = e.length; i < 25; i += 1) {
   175	    e.push(i % 2 === 0 ? evt({ type: 'decision', payload: { summary: `decision ${i}` } }, `e2e-d${i}`) : evt({ type: 'note', payload: { text: `note ${i}` } }, `e2e-n${i}`));
   176	  }
   177	  return e;
   178	}
   179	
   180	async function runCheckpoints(cwd, platform) {
   181	  for (const [i, stdin] of buildEvents().entries()) {
   182	    const io = makeRealIo(cwd, { stdin });
   183	    const code = await cmdCheckpoint(['--platform', platform], io);
   184	    assert.equal(code, 0, `checkpoint ${i} must be hook-safe (exit 0); stderr: ${io.stderrText()}`);
   185	  }
   186	}
   187	
   188	/** init a real repo, scaffold, then pin the controlled config (E3). */
   189	async function setupProject(prefix) {
   190	  const cwd = mkTmp(prefix);
   191	  initRepo(cwd);
   192	  const initIo = makeRealIo(cwd);
   193	  const initCode = await cmdInit([], initIo);
   194	  assert.equal(initCode, 0, `init must scaffold cleanly; stderr: ${initIo.stderrText()}`);
   195	  // init-scaffold correctness (E3): a valid config + a covering .gitignore.
   196	  assert.equal(JSON.parse(nodeFs.readFileSync(join(cwd, 'baton.config.json'), 'utf8')).schema, 'baton/config@1');
   197	  assert.match(nodeFs.readFileSync(join(cwd, '.gitignore'), 'utf8'), /(^|\n)\.handoff\/?\n/);
   198	  // Pin the controlled matrix for deterministic remap.
   199	  nodeFs.writeFileSync(join(cwd, 'baton.config.json'), JSON.stringify(CONFIG, null, 2));
   200	  return cwd;
   201	}
   202	
   203	/** Snapshot every file under <cwd>/.handoff for byte-level no-mutation checks. */
   204	function snapshotTree(cwd) {
   205	  const out = {};
   206	  const walk = (dir) => {
   207	    if (!nodeFs.existsSync(dir)) return;
   208	    for (const name of nodeFs.readdirSync(dir)) {
   209	      const p = join(dir, name);
   210	      if (nodeFs.statSync(p).isDirectory()) walk(p);
   211	      else out[p] = nodeFs.readFileSync(p, 'utf8');
   212	    }
   213	  };
   214	  walk(join(cwd, '.handoff'));
   215	  return out;
   216	}
   217	
   218	/**
   219	 * A directly-written bundle + config repo (no git needed) for contract tests.
   220	 * status 'sealed' models a finalized handoff; 'open' models the primary failure
   221	 * case — limit death before finalize (plan: open -> received, degraded seal).
   222	 */
   223	function writeBundleRepo(prefix, originPlatform, status = 'sealed', overrides = {}) {
   224	  const cwd = mkTmp(prefix);
   225	  const p = bundlePaths(cwd);
   226	  nodeFs.mkdirSync(p.dir, { recursive: true });
   227	  const bundle = {
   228	    schema: 'baton/bundle@1',
   229	    bundleId: 'b_e2edirect00000',
   230	    generation: 1,
   231	    createdAt: NOW,
   232	    updatedAt: NOW,
   233	    origin: { platform: originPlatform, model: 'm', sessionHint: 'orig-sess', unstable: false },
   234	    task: { goal: GOAL, constraints: [], acceptance: [] },
   235	    plan: { steps: [{ id: 's1', title: 'Continue the task', status: 'active', note: null }] },
   236	    decisions: [],
   237	    files: { touched: [] },
   238	    roles: { assignments: {} },
   239	    git: null,
   240	    handoff:
   241	      status === 'sealed'
   242	        ? { status: 'sealed', reason: 'seal', reasonClass: 'usage-limit', toPlatformHint: null, finalizedAt: NOW, receive_log: [] }
   243	        : { status: 'open', reason: null, reasonClass: null, toPlatformHint: null, finalizedAt: null, receive_log: [] },
   244	    journalSeq: 0,
   245	    compaction: { droppedDecisions: 0, note: null },
   246	    dedupeRing: [],
   247	    ...overrides,
   248	  };
   249	  nodeFs.writeFileSync(p.snapshot, JSON.stringify(bundle, null, 2) + '\n');
   250	  nodeFs.writeFileSync(join(cwd, 'baton.config.json'), JSON.stringify(CONFIG, null, 2));
   251	  return cwd;
   252	}
   253	
   254	const platformsOf = (assignments) => Object.values(assignments).map((a) => a.platform);
   255	
   256	const receiveFreezes = (cwd) => {
   257	  const dir = bundlePaths(cwd).historyDir;
   258	  if (!nodeFs.existsSync(dir)) return [];
   259	  return nodeFs.readdirSync(dir).filter((n) => /\.receive\.json$/.test(n));
   260	};

   279	
   280	  function smokeExecFile(rec) {
   281	    return (/** @type {string} */ cmd, /** @type {string[]} */ args = []) => {
   282	      rec.push([cmd, ...args].join(' '));
   283	      // git stays unavailable; the smoke command "passes".
   284	      if (cmd === 'git') return Promise.reject(Object.assign(new Error('spawn ENOENT'), { code: 'ENOENT' }));
   285	      return Promise.resolve({ stdout: 'ok\n', stderr: '' });
   286	    };
   287	  }

   369	describe('e2e failover — six-direction matrix, sealed AND open variants (prepare + commit)', () => {

   381	      it(`${origin} -> ${target} (${status}): no dead-origin remap; commit adopts ${target}, opens gen 2, archives a received${status === 'open' ? ' degraded' : ''} seal`, async () => {
   382	        const reason = LIMIT_FOR[origin];
   383	        const cwd = writeBundleRepo('baton-e2e-dir-', origin, status);

   388	        const data = JSON.parse(prep.stdoutText()).data;
   389

   391	        assert.ok(!platforms.includes(origin), `(E2) no role may remap back onto the dead origin ${origin}`);
   392	        assert.ok(platforms.some((pf) => pf !== null), 'at least one role resolves to a usable platform');
   393	        for (const [role, a] of Object.entries(data.assignments)) {
   394	          assert.ok(['native', 'delegated', 'unavailable'].includes(a.mode), `${role}.mode invalid: ${a.mode}`);

   397	          assert.ok(
   398	            data.warnings.some((w) => /unsealed|never finalized|degraded/i.test(w)),

   400	          );
   401	        }
   402

   404	        const commitIo = makeRealIo(cwd, { now: NOW });
   405	        const code = await cmdReceive(['--platform', target, '--commit', data.token, '--origin', origin, '--reason', reason, '--json'], commitIo);

   407

   409	        const live = loadBundle(cwd, makeRealIo(cwd)).bundle;
   410	        assert.equal(live.generation, 2, 'commit opened a fresh generation');
   411	        assert.equal(live.origin.platform, target, 'ownership adopted to the receiving platform');
   412	        assert.equal(live.handoff.status, 'open', 'the new live generation is writable (open)');
   413

   415	        const freezes = receiveFreezes(cwd);
   416	        assert.equal(freezes.length, 1, 'exactly one receive rotation freeze exists');
   417	        const freeze = JSON.parse(nodeFs.readFileSync(join(bundlePaths(cwd).historyDir, freezes[0]), 'utf8'));
   418	        assert.equal(freeze.handoff.status, 'received', 'the archived freeze records the received transition');
   419	        const entry = freeze.handoff.receive_log[freeze.handoff.receive_log.length - 1];
   420	        assert.ok(entry, 'the received seal logs the receive');
   421	        assert.equal(entry.origin, origin, 'the receive log records the intake origin');
   422	        assert.equal(entry.reason, reason, 'the receive log records the intake reason');
   423	
   424	        if (status === 'open') {
   425	          // The primary failure case: a receive-generated DEGRADED seal.
   426	          assert.match(JSON.stringify(entry), /degraded[-_ ]?seal|degradedSeal/i, 'an open->received commit flags the degraded seal');
   427	          assert.equal(freeze.handoff.reason, reason, 'the degraded seal records the intake reason as the seal reason');
   428	        } else {
   429	          assert.doesNotMatch(JSON.stringify(entry), /degraded/i, 'a sealed->received commit is NOT flagged degraded');
   430	        }

   547	        guardedWrite(cwd, io, token, () => {

   549	          nodeFs.writeFileSync(guardedTarget, '{"clobbered":true}');
   550	        });
   551	      } catch (e) {
   552	        fenceError = e;
   553	      }
   554	    });

     1	/**
     2	 * Child supervision — the loop's reliability surface. Owns the FULL child
     3	 * lifecycle: argv assembly (per-role permissions), detached process-group
     4	 * spawn with closed stdin, timeout SIGTERM→grace→SIGKILL of the whole group,
     5	 * capped transcript logs, region-bounded verdict parsing (prompt-echo-proof),
     6	 * and exit classification via core detect. Plan:
     7	 * docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md §"Child supervision
     8	 * contract".

    10	import { spawn } from 'node:child_process';
    11	import { mkdirSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs';

    13	import { classify } from '../detect/classifier.mjs';
    14	
    15	// Every review seat is read-only — writers are the implementer-side roles
    16	// only. The merger CHILD is read-only too: it adversarially re-checks, but
    17	// the SUPERVISOR performs the actual merge via worktrees.mergeSubtask
    18	// (pipeline contract — the merger child never issues git).
    19	const REVIEWER_ROLES = new Set(['plan-reviewer', 'test-verifier', 'subtask-reviewer', 'final-reviewer-a', 'final-reviewer-b', 'merger']);
    20	
    21	// AGENTS.md §9: children commit solely as the repo owner, zero AI attribution.
    22	const GIT_IDENTITY = Object.freeze({
    23	  GIT_AUTHOR_NAME: 'SakshamUboweja',
    24	  GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL: 'ssakshamu@gmail.com',
    25	  GIT_COMMITTER_NAME: 'SakshamUboweja',
    26	  GIT_COMMITTER_EMAIL: 'ssakshamu@gmail.com',
    27	});
    28	
    29	/**
    30	 * Assemble the spawn contract for a role child. Reviewers are read-only on
    31	 * every platform; every child is marked supervised (its own baton hooks
    32	 * no-op), carries the sole-author git identity, and has stdin CLOSED at
    33	 * spawn (the codex-exec stdin-hang field failure).
    34	 * @param {{platform: string, role: string, model: string, effort?: string | null, mode?: string}} assignment
    35	 * @param {string} prompt
    36	 * @param {{root: string}} opts
    37	 * @returns {{command: string, args: string[], env: Record<string, string>, stdio: any[]}}
    38	 */
    39	export function buildChildArgv(assignment, prompt, opts) {
    40	  const readOnly = REVIEWER_ROLES.has(assignment.role);
    41	  const env = { BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD: '1', ...GIT_IDENTITY };
    42	  const stdio = ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'];
    43	
    44	  if (assignment.platform === 'claude-code') {
    45	    const args = ['-p', prompt];
    46	    if (readOnly) {
    47	      args.push('--permission-mode', 'default', '--allowedTools', 'Read,Grep,Glob');
    48	    } else {
    49	      args.push('--permission-mode', 'acceptEdits', '--allowedTools', 'Read,Grep,Glob,Write,Edit,Bash');
    50	    }
    51	    return { command: 'claude', args, env, stdio };
    52	  }
    53	
    54	  // codex (and the default shape for exec-style CLIs).
    55	  const args = ['exec', '-C', opts.root, '-s', readOnly ? 'read-only' : 'workspace-write', '--model', assignment.model];
    56	  if (assignment.effort) args.push('-c', `model_reasoning_effort=${assignment.effort}`);
    57	  args.push(prompt);
    58	  return { command: 'codex', args, env, stdio };
    59	}
    60	
    61	const VERDICT_RE = /VERDICT:\s*(APPROVED_WITH_NOTES|APPROVED|BLOCKED)\b/g;
    62	
    63	/** @param {string} region @returns {{verdict: string, findings: string} | null} */
    64	function lastVerdictIn(region) {
    65	  let last = null;
    66	  for (const m of region.matchAll(VERDICT_RE)) last = m;
    67	  if (!last) return null;
    68	  const after = region.slice((last.index ?? 0) + last[0].length);
    69	  return { verdict: last[1], findings: after.trim() };
    70	}
    71	
    72	/**
    73	 * Region-bounded verdict parsing — the prompt region (which quotes the
    74	 * verdict format in its instructions) is NEVER scanned, and there is no
    75	 * whole-transcript fallback. Unparseable is BLOCKED, never APPROVED.
    76	 * - codex: only the text after the LAST line starting with 'tokens used'.
    77	 * - claude-code: only the `result` field of the structured JSON output.
    78	 * Multiple tails inside the trusted region → the LAST one wins.
    79	 * @param {string} transcript @param {{platform: string}} opts
    80	 * @returns {{verdict: string, findings: string, reason?: string}}
    81	 */
    82	export function parseVerdict(transcript, { platform }) {
    83	  const unparseable = { verdict: 'BLOCKED', findings: '', reason: 'unparseable' };
    84	  const text = String(transcript ?? '');
    85	
    86	  if (platform === 'claude-code') {
    87	    let obj = null;
    88	    try {
    89	      obj = JSON.parse(text.trim());
    90	    } catch {
    91	      return unparseable;
    92	    }
    93	    if (typeof obj?.result !== 'string') return unparseable;
    94	    const hit = lastVerdictIn(obj.result);
    95	    return hit ?? unparseable;
    96	  }
    97	
    98	  // codex: locate the LAST 'tokens used' marker line; the trusted region is
    99	  // strictly after it.
   100	  const lines = text.split('\n');
   101	  let markerLine = -1;
   102	  for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i += 1) {
   103	    if (/^tokens used\b/i.test(lines[i])) markerLine = i;
   104	  }
   105	  if (markerLine === -1) return unparseable;
   106	  const region = lines.slice(markerLine + 1).join('\n');
   107	  const hit = lastVerdictIn(region);
   108	  return hit ?? unparseable;
   109	}
   110	
   111	const TRUNCATION_MARKER = '\n[... truncated by baton log cap ...]\n';
   112	
   113	/**
   114	 * Cap a transcript: head preserved, middle replaced with a marker, TAIL
   115	 * preserved verbatim (the verdict lives at the end and must never be cut).
   116	 * @param {string} text @param {number} maxBytes
   117	 * @returns {string}
   118	 */
   119	export function capLog(text, maxBytes) {
   120	  const s = String(text ?? '');
   121	  if (Buffer.byteLength(s) <= maxBytes) return s;
   122	  const headBytes = Math.max(1, Math.floor(maxBytes * 0.4));
   123	  const tailBytes = Math.max(1, maxBytes - headBytes);
   124	  const buf = Buffer.from(s);
   125	  const head = buf.subarray(0, headBytes).toString();
   126	  const tail = buf.subarray(buf.length - tailBytes).toString();
   127	  return head + TRUNCATION_MARKER + tail;
   128	}
   129	
   130	/**
   131	 * Exit classification delegates to core detect — one classifier, one
   132	 * signature table, no drift. Returns exactly classify(...).class.
   133	 * @param {string} transcript @param {number} exitCode @param {string} platform @param {any} table
   134	 * @returns {string}
   135	 */
   136	export function classifyChildExit(transcript, exitCode, platform, table) {
   137	  return classify({ text: transcript, exitCode, platform, table }).class;
   138	}
   139	
   140	/** @param {number} pid @param {NodeJS.Signals} sig */
   141	function killGroup(pid, sig) {
   142	  try {
   143	    process.kill(-pid, sig);
   144	  } catch {
   145	    // ESRCH — already gone.
   146	  }
   147	}
   148	
   149	/**
   150	 * Spawn and supervise one child: detached in its OWN process group, stdin
   151	 * closed; on timeout SIGTERM the group, wait graceMs, SIGKILL the group (no
   152	 * zombie grandchildren). Output streams to a capped log at opts.logPath; the
   153	 * verdict is parsed from the full transcript, region-bounded.
   154	 * @param {{command: string, args: string[], env?: Record<string, string>, cwd?: string}} spec
   155	 * @param {{timeoutMs: number, graceMs: number, logPath: string, maxLogBytes?: number, platform?: string}} opts
   156	 * @returns {Promise<{timedOut: boolean, exitCode: number | null, verdict?: string, findings?: string, reason?: string, logPath: string}>}
   157	 */
   158	export function superviseChild(spec, opts) {
   159	  return new Promise((resolve) => {
   160	    const maxLogBytes = opts.maxLogBytes ?? 1_000_000;
   161	    const child = spawn(spec.command, spec.args, {
   162	      cwd: spec.cwd,
   163	      env: { ...process.env, ...(spec.env ?? {}) },
   164	      stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'],
   165	      detached: true, // its own process group — the kill unit
   166	    });
   167	
   168	    let out = '';
   169	    let timedOut = false;
   170	    /** @type {NodeJS.Timeout | null} */
   171	    let killTimer = null;
   172	
   173	    const onChunk = (/** @type {Buffer} */ c) => {
   174	      out += c.toString();
   175	    };
   176	    child.stdout?.on('data', onChunk);
   177	    child.stderr?.on('data', onChunk);
   178	
   179	    const timeout = setTimeout(() => {
   180	      timedOut = true;
   181	      if (typeof child.pid === 'number') {
   182	        killGroup(child.pid, 'SIGTERM');
   183	        killTimer = setTimeout(() => {
   184	          if (typeof child.pid === 'number') killGroup(child.pid, 'SIGKILL');
   185	        }, opts.graceMs);
   186	      }
   187	    }, opts.timeoutMs);
   188	
   189	    const finish = (/** @type {number | null} */ exitCode) => {
   190	      clearTimeout(timeout);
   191	      if (killTimer) clearTimeout(killTimer);
   192	      // Belt-and-braces: reap any group survivors even on a clean exit path.
   193	      if (timedOut && typeof child.pid === 'number') killGroup(child.pid, 'SIGKILL');
   194	      try {
   195	        mkdirSync(dirname(opts.logPath), { recursive: true });
   196	        writeFileSync(opts.logPath, capLog(out, maxLogBytes));
   197	      } catch {
   198	        // The log must never mask the result.
   199	      }
   200	      const parsed = parseVerdict(out, { platform: opts.platform ?? 'codex' });
   201	      resolve({
   202	        timedOut,
   203	        exitCode: timedOut ? null : exitCode,
   204	        verdict: parsed.verdict,
   205	        findings: parsed.findings,
   206	        ...(parsed.reason ? { reason: parsed.reason } : {}),
   207	        logPath: opts.logPath,
   208	      });
   209	    };
   210	
   211	    child.on('error', () => finish(null));
   212	    child.on('close', (code) => finish(code));
   213	  });
   214	}
